href="../sources/dabeka2003-canadian-total-diet-mercury">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported lead in frankfurter, cooked from 7 results in 1 reporting region, with a subgroup median of 3.2 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> In the US Total Diet Study, frankfurter, cooked had a median lead result below 4 µg/kg across 22 composites from 2017–2020.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Across 2 other studies of ordinary farms and markets, reported lead means and medians on an as-sold or fresh-weight basis ranged from 3 µg/kg (Spungen et al. 2024) to 5 µg/kg (Korfali et al. 2013).<sup class="cite">[[sources/korfali2013-lebanon-canned-foods-metals|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Korfali SI, Abou Hamdan W · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/korfali2013-lebanon-canned-foods-metals">Essentail and Toxic Metals in Lebanese Marketed Canned Food: Impact of Metal Cans</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Food Research 2(1) (2013) 19-30</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/korfali2013-lebanon-canned-foods-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For cadmium, 2 studies on an as-sold or fresh-weight basis reported means and medians from 2.9 µg/kg (Spungen et al. 2024) to 29 µg/kg (Korfali et al. 2013).<sup class="cite">[[sources/korfali2013-lebanon-canned-foods-metals|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Korfali SI, Abou Hamdan W · 2013</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/korfali2013-lebanon-canned-foods-metals">Essentail and Toxic Metals in Lebanese Marketed Canned Food: Impact of Metal Cans</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Food Research 2(1) (2013) 19-30</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/korfali2013-lebanon-canned-foods-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the frankfurter came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.



<a id="ingredient-identity-and-scope"></a>
## What counts as frankfurter

<a id="hmi-ing-001"></a>
<a id="what-commodity-biological-species-edible-part-and-commercial-forms-does-this-page-cover"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-002"></a>
<a id="which-processing-states-and-derivatives-are-distinct-materials-rather-than-synonyms"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-003"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-can-be-transferred-between-those-materials-and-what-requires-a-separate-answer"></a>

This page covers frankfurter in 3 forms kept separate in every comparison: frankfurter, canned (27 results), frankfurter, cooked (26 results) and frankfurter, as sold (12 results).<sup <div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-frankfurter" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-frankfurter-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of frankfurter, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

Frankfurter, cooked; As sold, as eaten or fresh weight, µg/kg; no moisture conversion. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>≤3.5</td><td>3–4 (bounded)</td><td>4.35</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>≤3.5</td><td>3–4 (bounded)</td><td>4.35</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>24 reference units; 2 sampling families. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>WHO GEMS/Food</td><td>2</td><td>50%</td></tr><tr><td>FDA Total Diet Study</td><td>22</td><td>50%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for frankfurter, cooked on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>2 sampling families with different designs; none is a probability sample of world supply. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" class="internal" data-slug="sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" data-no-cite-meta>WHO GEMS/Food</a></li><li><a href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020" class="internal" data-slug="sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020" data-no-cite-meta>FDA Total Diet Study</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. <a id="sources-and-contamination-pathways"></a>
## How metals reach frankfurter

<a id="hmi-ing-010"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-root-uptake-surface-deposition-feed-transfer-or-other-biological-pathways"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-011"></a>
<a id="what-contamination-can-arise-from-agricultural-inputs-harvesting-processing-equipment-or-packaging"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-012"></a>
<a id="which-source-attribution-claims-are-measured-modelled-inferred-or-unresolved"></a>

No study in the library compared frankfurter from contaminated and ordinary settings, so the routes by which metals reach it are not measured here.

<a id="processing-effects"></a>
<a id="ingredient-derivative-risk"></a>
<a id="processing-effects-and-derivatives"></a>
## Processing, cooking and preparation

<a id="hmi-ing-016"></a>
<a id="how-do-specified-processing-or-preparation-steps-change-the-measured-metal-endpoints"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-017"></a>
<a id="how-do-derivatives-concentrates-extracts-and-fractions-differ-from-their-parent-ingredient"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-018"></a>
<a id="do-changes-reflect-removal-redistribution-moisture-or-yield-change-dilution-or-altered-species-or-availability"></a>

Processed or prepared forms were compared only with unprocessed frankfurter in the same study and group. Frankfurter, cooked (PAHO) had cadmium higher at 13.2 µg/kg, against 4 µg/kg for the unprocessed form in the same group (frankfurter, as sold).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Forms differ in water content, so a per-kilogram difference is not by itself a measured removal or gain of metal.



<a id="mitigation-options"></a>
## Mitigation for frankfurter

<a id="hmi-ing-019"></a>
<a id="which-prevention-mitigation-or-remediation-measures-have-been-evaluated-for-this-ingredient"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-020"></a>
<a id="under-what-conditions-at-which-stage-and-against-which-comparator-do-they-work-or-fail"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-021"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-field-or-commercial-implementation-and-ongoing-verification"></a>

Lot testing against the applicable limit is the check that can be verified.

<p class="hmi-mitigation-gap" data-mitigation-gap>No measured way to lower metals in this food is in the library yet.</p>



<a id="trade-offs-and-failure-modes"></a>
## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="hmi-ing-022"></a>
<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-023"></a>
<a id="what-effects-on-nutrition-yield-sensory-quality-stability-and-waste-streams-were-measured"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-024"></a>
<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes a lead limit says little about cadmium, arsenic or nickel unless those are tested as well.



<a id="sampling-and-testing"></a>
## Testing frankfurter for metals

<a id="hmi-ing-025"></a>
<a id="what-sampling-design-is-appropriate-for-the-ingredient-heterogeneity-lot-and-decision"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-026"></a>
<a id="which-methods-species-measurements-validation-evidence-and-reporting-limits-are-needed"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-027"></a>
<a id="what-can-a-certificate-of-analysis-or-non-detect-establish-and-what-remains-untested"></a>

Results depend on the method and its reporting limit. Reporting limits for lead ranged from 0.4 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 3,000 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a →</a></span></span></sup> Reporting limits for cadmium ranged from 1 µg/kg (FDA Total Diet Study) to 7 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup A useful lot result names the form, the basis and a quantification limit well below the limit that applies.



<a id="procurement-and-process-control"></a>
## Buying and verifying frankfurter

No maximum level for metals in frankfurter was verified from primary text for this page; buyers should confirm the limit that applies in the destination market.



<a id="finished-product-and-exposure-implications"></a>
## Exposure from frankfurter

<a id="hmi-ing-031"></a>
<a id="how-do-inclusion-rate-processing-yield-preparation-and-other-ingredients-affect-a-finished-product-estimate"></a>

No source in the library converted frankfurter concentrations into a verified estimate of intake for a defined population. Concentrations describe the food, not individual risk.

<a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## Frankfurter FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-frankfurter" data-anchor="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-frankfurter" data-question="What heavy metals are found in frankfurter?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What heavy metals are found in frankfurter?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The library reports lead, cadmium, total arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, tin, uranium, Cu, Fe, Zn and Se in frankfurter, most often lead, cadmium and total arsenic.<sup →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug
